> देवानां । यः । इत् । मनः । यजमानः । इयक्षति । अभि । इत् । आयज्वनः ।  
> भुवत् ॥ १७ ॥

### Printed p. 221

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यो यजमानो देवानां मनो यष्टुमिच्छति तं नकिर्न कश्चिदपि नशत् ।
> स्वकीयॆन कर्मणा न व्याप्नोति । नशतिर्वाप्तिकर्मा । किंच स यष्टा न प्र योषत् ।
> स्वस्मात्स्थानान्न विभक्तः पृथक्कृतो न भवति । किंच न योषति । पुत्रादिभिर्धनादिभिश्च
> न विभक्तो भवति ॥ यु मिश्रणामिश्रणयोरित्यस्योभयत्र लेट्यडागमः ॥ शिष्टं
> व्याख्यातं ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Whatever sacrificer wishes to worship the mind of the gods, no one at all can reach him by his own act; the root used here has the sense of reaching. Moreover, that sacrificer is not driven from his place; he is not separated or set apart. And he is not separated from sons and the rest, or from wealth and the rest. The remainder has been explained.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—the sacrificer who
- देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—of the gods
- मनः इत् (*manaḥ it*)—the mind itself, aiming at it
- इयक्षति (*iyakṣati*)—will satisfy
- तं (*taṃ*)—that sacrificer
- कर्मणा (*karmaṇā*)—by his own deeds
- नकिः (*nakiḥ*)—no one at all
- नशत् (*naśat*)—can overpower
- न प्र योषत् (*na pra yoṣat*)—such a sacrificer does not fall from his place
- न योषति (*na yoṣati*)—he is not separated from sons and the rest
- सः (*saḥ*) यजमानः (*yajamānaḥ*)—that same sacrificer
- अयज्जनः (*ayajjanaḥ*)—the people fallen from rites
- अभि भुवत् इत् (*abhi bhuvat it*)—certainly overcomes

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

No one can overpower the sacrificer who satisfies the very mind of the gods by worship. He does not fall from his place and is not separated from sons and the rest. That same sacrificer certainly overcomes the people fallen from rites.

##### English translation
No one obstructs him by his acts, he is never driven (from his station), he is never separated (from his family): the celebrator of the sacrifice, who desires to propitiate the mind of the gods, overcomes those who are no sacrificers.

#### Ṛk 18

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अस॒दत्र॑ सु॒वीर्य॑मु॒त त्यदा॒श्वश्वं ॑।  
> दे॒वानां॒ य इन्मनो॒ यज॑मान॒ इय॑क्षत्य॒भीदय॑ज्जनो  
> भुवत् ॥ १८ ॥

### Printed p. 222

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आसत् । अत्र । सुवीर्यं । उत । त्यत् । आशुअश्वं ।  
> देवानां । यः । इत् । मनः । यजमानः । इयक्षति । अभि । इति । आयज्वनः ।  
> भुवत् ॥ १८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यो यजमानो देवानां मनो यष्टुमिच्छति अत्रास्मिन्यजमाने सुवीर्यं
> शोभनवीर्योपेतं पुत्रादिकमसत् । भवत्येव । उतापि चाश्वश्वमाशुगमनाश्वसंघयुक्तं त्यत्तद्धनादिकं तस्मिन्यजमाने भवति । देनानां मनो यो जनो
> हविर्भिर्यष्टुमिच्छति यद्वा पूजयितुमिच्छति स जनोऽयज्वनः सर्वानभिभवत्येव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The sacrificer who wishes to worship the mind of the gods will certainly have in him sons and the rest endowed with fine strength. And that famous wealth and the like, together with troops of swift-going horses, will be with that sacrificer. The man who wishes to worship the mind of the gods with oblations, or else to honour it, overcomes all who perform no sacrifice.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—the sacrificer who
- देवानां (*devānāṃ*)—of the gods
- मन इत् (*mana it*)—the mind itself
- इयक्षति (*iyakṣati*)—worships and satisfies
- अत्र (*atra*)—in that same sacrificer
- सुवीर्यः (*suvīryaḥ*)—sons and the rest endowed with excellent strength
- आसत् (*āsat*)—are certain to be
- उत (*uta*)—and
- त्यत् (*tyat*)—famous
- आश्वश्वं (*āśvaśvaṃ*)—wealth provided with swift-going horses is also there
- सः (*saḥ*) यजमानः (*yajamānaḥ*)—that same sacrificer
- आयज्वनः (*āyajvanaḥ*)—those without rites
- अभि भुवत् इत् (*abhi bhuvat it*)—certainly overcomes

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The sacrificer who worships and satisfies the very mind of the gods will certainly have in him sons and the rest endowed with excellent strength. And he will have famous wealth, accompanied by swift-going horses. That same sacrificer certainly overcomes the people fallen from rites.

##### English translation
To him in this life is a valiant progeny, to him are swift herds of horses; the celebrator of the sacrifice, who desires to propitiate the mind of the gods, overcomes those who are no sacrificers.

### Printed p. 223

वेदार्थस्य प्रकाशेन तमो हार्दं निवारयन् । पुमर्थांश्च तुरो देयाद्विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरः ॥ (*vedārthasya prakāśena tamo hārdaṃ nivārayan | pumarthāṃśca turo deyādvidyātīrthamaheśvaraḥ ‖*)

इति श्रीमद्राजाधिराज परमेश्वर वैदिकमार्गप्रवर्तक श्री वीरबुक्कभूपालसाम्राज्यधुरंधरेण सायणाचार्येण विरचिते माधवीये वेदार्थप्रकाशे ऋक्संहिताभाष्ये पष्माष्टके द्वितीयोऽध्यायः समाप्तः ॥ (*iti śrīmadrājādhirāja parameśvara vaidikamārgapravartaka śrī vīrabukkabhūpālasāmrājyadhuraṃdhareṇa sāyaṇācāryeṇa viracite mādhavīye vedārthaprakāśe ṛksaṃhitābhāṣye paṣmāṣṭake dvitīyo 'dhyāyaḥ samāptaḥ ‖*)

ओं तत्त्वत् (*oṃ tattvat*)

Here ends the second adhyāya in the sixth aṣṭaka of the Ṛgveda-saṃhitā, with the bhāṣya of Śrī Sāyaṇa and with the Karnāṭaka-language translation.

यदक्षरपदभ्रष्टं मात्राहीनं तु यद्भवेत् । तत्सर्वं क्षम्यतां देव वागीश्वर नमोऽस्तुते ॥ (*yadakṣarapadabhraṣṭaṃ mātrāhīnaṃ tu yadbhavet | tatsarvaṃ kṣamyatāṃ deva vāgīśvara namo 'stute ‖*)

शुभं भूयात् (*śubhaṃ bhūyāt*)

ओं (*oṃ*) **NOTE**

श्री गणाधिपतये नमः (*śrī gaṇādhipataye namaḥ*)

वागीशाद्याः सुमनसः सर्वार्थानामुपक्रमे । यं नत्वा कृतकृत्याः स्युस्तं नमामि गजाननं । (*vāgīśādyāḥ sumanasaḥ sarvārthānāmupakrame | yaṃ natvā kṛtakṛtyāḥ syustaṃ namāmi gajānanaṃ |*)

ं । यस्य निश्वसितं वेदा यो वेदेभ्योऽखिलं जगत् । निर्ममे तमहं वंदे विद्यातीर्थमहेश्वरं । (*ṃ | yasya niśvasitaṃ vedā yo vedebhyo 'khilaṃ jagat | nirmame tamahaṃ vaṃde vidyātīrthamaheśvaraṃ |*)

ं । वेदः शिवः शिवो वेदो वेदाध्यायी सदाशिवः । तस्मात्सर्वप्रयत्नेन वेदमेव सदा जपेत् ॥ (*ṃ | vedaḥ śivaḥ śivo vedo vedādhyāyī sadāśivaḥ | tasmātsarvaprayatnena vedameva sadā japet ‖*)

Ṛgveda-saṃhitā with the bhāṣya of Sāyaṇa (with the explanation of the meaning in the Karnāṭaka language). The third adhyāya in the sixth aṣṭaka. The third adhyāya in the sixth aṣṭaka; the thirty-second sūkta in the eighth maṇḍala.

**NOTE**

## Sūkta 32 (Maṇḍala 8)

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अथ षष्ठस्य तृतीयोऽध्याय आरभ्यते ॥ प्र कृतानीति त्रिंचदृचं द्वितीयं
> सूक्तं । तत्रेयमनुक्रमणिका । प्र कृतानि त्रिंशन्मेधातिथिरिति । काणो मेधातिथिर्यषिः । परं गायत्रं प्राग्वत्सप्रेरिति परिभाषया गायत्री छंदः । अनादेशपरिभाषयेंद्रो देवता ॥ महाव्रते निष्केवल्ये गायत्रतृचाशीतावेतत्सूक्तं । तथैव
> पंचमारण्यके शौनकेन सूत्रितं । प्र कृतान्यजीषिण आ घा ये अग्निमिंधते ।
> ऐ. आ. ५-२-३ । इति ॥ अतिरात्रे प्रथमे पर्याये मैत्रावरुणशस्त्रे प्र कृतानीति
> तृचोऽनुरूपः । सूत्रितं च । प्र व इंद्राय मादनं प्र कृतान्यजीषणः । आ. ६-४ ।

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Now the third adhyāya of the sixth aṣṭaka begins. The second sūkta, beginning प्र कृतानि (*pra kṛtāni*), has thirty Ṛks. The Anukramaṇikā says प्र कृतानि त्रिंशन्मेधातिथिरिति (*pra kṛtāni triṃśanmedhātithiriti*): Medhātithi Kāṇva is the seer. The metre is Gāyatrī by a rule of the Anukramaṇikā, and the deity is Indra by the rule of no specification. In the Mahāvrata, in the Niṣkevalya rite, this sūkta is used for the Gāyatra tṛcas. **NOTE** Likewise Śaunaka, in the fifth Āraṇyaka, has given the rule प्र कृतान्यजीषिण आ घा ये अग्निमिंधते (*pra kṛtānyajīṣiṇa ā ghā ye agnimiṃdhate*) (Ai. Ā. 5-2-3). In the Atirātra, in the first paryāya, in the Maitrāvaruṇa śastra, the tṛca beginning प्र कृतानि (*pra kṛtāni*) is the anurūpa; and it is given in the rule प्र व इंद्राय मादनं प्र कृतान्यजीषणः (*pra va iṃdrāya mādanaṃ pra kṛtānyajīṣaṇaḥ*) (Ā. 6-4).

### Printed p. 226

> इति ॥ तस्मिन्नेव शस्त्रे प्रति श्रुतायेत्याद्याः पंचदशर्चः । सूत्र्यते हि । प्रति
> श्रुताय वो धृषदिति पंचदश । आ. ६-४ । इति ॥ दशमेऽहनि प्रातःसवनेऽच्छावाकनादे पत्नीयजमानस्थाने प्रति श्रुतायेति तृचः । सूत्रितं च । प्रति श्रुताय
> वो धृषदिति तृचौ । आ. ८-१२ इति ॥ अहीनांतर्गतस्यातिरात्रस्य प्रथमे पर्याये
> होतुः पन्य इदुप गायतेत्यनुरूपः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In that same śastra, fifteen Ṛks beginning प्रति श्रुताय (*prati śrutāya*) are used; it is given in the rule प्रति श्रुताय वो धृषदिति पंचदश (*prati śrutāya vo dhṛṣaditi paṃcadaśa*) (Ā. 6-4). On the tenth day, at the morning pressing, in the place of the wife of the sacrificer in the Acchāvāka's recitation, the tṛca beginning प्रति श्रुताय (*prati śrutāya*) is used; and it is given in the rule प्रति श्रुताय वो धृषदिति तृचौ (*prati śrutāya vo dhṛṣaditi tṛcau*) (Ā. 8-12). In the first paryāya of the Atirātra that belongs among the Ahīnas, for the Hotṛ, the anurūpa is पन्य इदुप गायतेत्यनुरूपः (*panya idupa gāyatetyanurūpaḥ*).

##### Anuvāda

Now the third adhyāya in the sixth aṣṭaka is begun. This sūkta beginning प्र कृतानि (*pra kṛtāni*) is the second sūkta in the fifth anuvāka of the eighth maṇḍala. It has thirty Ṛks. As the Anukramaṇikā says प्र कृतानि त्रिंशन्मेधातिथिः (*pra kṛtāni triṃśanmedhātithiḥ*): for this sūkta Medhātithi Kāṇva is the seer, Indra is the deity, and the metre is Gāyatrī. In the Mahāvrata rite, at the time of reciting the mantras of the Niṣkevalya śastra, this sūkta has its use for the Ṛks of the Gāyatrī metre; this is pointed out in the fifth Āraṇyaka of the Aitareya Āraṇyaka by the sentence प्र कृतान्वयजीषिण आ घा ये अग्निमिंधते (*pra kṛtānvayajīṣiṇa ā ghā ye agnimiṃdhate*) (Ai. Ā. 5-2-3). In the first paryāya of the sacrifice called Atirātra, in the śastra mantras recited by the priest called Maitrāvaruṇa, the three Ṛks beginning प्र कृतानि (*pra kṛtāni*) are to be recited as the anurūpa tṛca; this is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra प्र व इंद्राय मादनं प्र कृतान्वयजीषिणः (*pra va iṃdrāya mādanaṃ pra kṛtānvayajīṣiṇaḥ*) (Ā. 6-4). And the use, in the śastra mantras mentioned above, of the fifteen Ṛks of this sūkta beginning प्रति श्रुताय (*prati śrutāya*) (4-18) is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra प्रति श्रुताय वो धृषदिति पंचदश (*prati śrutāya vo dhṛṣaditi paṃcadaśa*) (Ā. 6-4). And on the tenth day of the sacrifice, at the time of the morning pressing, the three Ṛks beginning प्रति श्रुताय (*prati śrutāya*) (Ṛks 4-6) are to be recited for the Ṛk which the priest called Acchāvāka recites on behalf of the wife of the sacrificer; this is explained by the sūtra of the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra प्रति श्रुताय वो धृषदिति तृचौ (*prati śrutāya vo dhṛṣaditi tṛcau*) (Ā. 8-12). And in the middle of the Ahīna sūkta recited in the sacrifice called Atirātra, in the first paryāya, the priest called Hotṛ is to recite as the anurūpa tṛca the three Ṛks (Ṛks 17-19) beginning पन्य इदुप गायत (*panya idupa gāyata*); this is pointed out.

##### Particulars of the sūkta

- Maṇḍala 8; Anuvāka 5; Sūkta 32
- Aṣṭaka 6; Adhyāya 3; Vargas 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6
- Number of Ṛks in the sūkta: 30
- Seer: मेधातिथिः काण्णः (*medhātithiḥ kāṇṇaḥ*)
- Deity: इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)
- Metre: गायत्री (*gāyatrī*)

### Printed p. 227

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र कृ॒तान्य॑जी॒षिणः॒ कण्वा॒ इंद्र॑स्य॒ गाथ॑या ।  
> मदे॒ सोम॑स्य वोचत ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । कृतानि । ऋजीषिणः । कण्वाः । इंद्रस्य । गाथया ।  
> मदे । सोमस्य । वोचत ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे कण्वाः ऋजीषिण ऋजीषवतः सोमस्य कृतानि कर्माणेंद्रस्य गाथयेंद्रस्य वाचा मदेऽस्य मदे संजाते सति प्र वोचत । प्रब्रूत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Kaṇvas, proclaim the deeds of the soma that has residue, the deeds of the soma, with the song of Indra, that is, with speech about Indra, when his exhilaration has arisen.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- कण्वाः (*kaṇvāḥ*)—O seers of the Kaṇva lineage
- इंद्रस्य (*iṃdrasya*)—for Indra
- मदे (*made*)—when joy has arisen from the drinking of soma
- यजीषिणः (*yajīṣiṇaḥ*)—pressed again from soma plants already crushed and pressed once
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—of the soma juice
- कृतानि (*kṛtāni*)—the qualities such as the power to gladden
- गाथया (*gāthayā*)—with a sentence in the form of praise
- प्रवोचक (*pravocaka*)—praise

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O seers born in the lineage of Kaṇva, when Indra has been gladdened by drinking soma, praise well the surpassing qualities of the soma juice drawn by crushing yet once more the same soma plants that were crushed and pressed before.

##### English translation
Celebrate with songs the great deeds of Indra, the drinker of the stale Soma, when in his exhilaration.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

ऋजीषिणः सोमस्य (*ṛjīṣiṇaḥ somasya*)—Ṛjīṣa means the crushed plants that remain like husk after the soma juice has been pressed out. At the time of the third pressing, together with the juice obtained by crushing once more the plants already crushed once, any one of milk, curds and barley flour is mixed

### Printed p. 228

and offered to Indra. Therefore, at the time of the third pressing, when Indra has been gladdened by soma juice of this kind, the seer, addressing the seers of the Kaṇva lineage who are his own descendants, has said: praise well the surpassing qualities of that soma juice, which produce his gladness (mada).

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यः सृबिं॑द॒मन॑र्शनिं॒ पिपुं॑ दा॒सम॑ही॒शुवं॑ ।  
> वधी॑दु॒ग्रो रि॒णन्न॒ पः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । सृबिंदं । आनर्शनिं । पिपुं । दासं । अहीशुवं ।  
> वधीत । उग्रः । रिणन् । अपः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> य इंद्र उग्र उद्धूर्णस्तेजस्वी वा सोऽप उदकानि रिणन् प्रेरयन्
> सृबिंदनामकं शत्रुमनर्शनिमनर्शनिनामकं पिपुं पिपुनामकं च दासं जाहीशुवं च शत्रुं वधीत् । अवधीत् । जघान ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, who is fierce, that is, mighty or glorious, sending forth the waters, slew the enemy named Sṛbinda, the one named Anarśani, the one named Pipu, and the servile enemy Ahīśuva. The verb used means he slew, he killed. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Indra
- उग्रः (*ugraḥ*)—who is fierce and valiant, that same Indra
- अपः (*apaḥ*)—the waters
- रिणन् (*riṇan*)—sending forth
- सृबिंदं (*sṛbiṃdaṃ*)—the enemy named Sṛbinda
- अनर्शनिं (*anarśaniṃ*)—the one called Anarśani
- पिपुं (*pipuṃ*)—the one called Pipu
- दासं (*dāsaṃ*)—the servant
- अहीशुवं (*ahīśuvaṃ*)—the one called Ahīśuva
- वधीत् (*vadhīt*)—killed

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, who is cruel and valiant, pouring down rain-waters in abundance, killed the enemies called Sṛbinda, Anarśani, Pipu, Dāsa and Ahīśuva.

##### English translation
The fierce (deity) who, liberating the waters, has slain Sribinda, Anarshani, Pipru and the slave Ahishuva.

### Printed p. 229

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न्यर्बु॑दस्य वि॒ष्टपं॑ व॒र्ष्णाणं॑ बृह॒तस्ति॑र ।  
> कृ॒षे तदिं॑द्र॒ पौंस्यं॑ ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नि । अर्बुदस्य । विष्टपं । वर्ष्णाणं । बृहतः । तिर ।  
> कृषे । तत् । इंद्र । पौंस्यं ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र बृहतो महतोऽर्बुदस्य मेघस्य वर्ष्णाणमुदकस्य वारकं
> विष्टपं स्थानं नि तिर । विध्य । तत्प्रसिद्धं पौंस्यं व्रतं च कृषे । कुरु ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, pierce the place of the great Arbuda, that is, of the cloud, the place that holds back the water. And perform that famous manly deed, and the vow.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- बृहतः (*bṛhataḥ*)—very great
- अर्बुदस्य (*arbudasya*)—of the cloud
- वर्षाणं (*varṣāṇaṃ*)—that holds back the water
- विष्टपं (*viṣṭapaṃ*)—the place
- नितिर (*nitira*)—break open
- तत् (*tat*)—that famous
- पौंस्यं (*pauṃsyaṃ*)—deed of daring
- कृषे (*kṛṣe*)—do

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, split the cloud that has spread out in vast form and holds back the water without pouring it down, and pour out rain. Do this deed of daring yourself.

##### English translation
Pierce the rain-holding domain of the vast Arbuda : achieve, Indra, this manly exploit.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्रति॑ श्रु॒ताय॑ वो धृ॒षत्तूर्णा॑शं॒ न गि॒रेरधि॑ ।  
> हु॒वे सु॑शि॒प्रमू॒तये॑ ॥ ४ ॥

### Printed p. 230

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्रति । श्रुताय । वः । धृषत् । तोर्णाशं । न । गिरेः । अधि ।  
> हुवे । सुशिप्रं । ऊतये ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे स्तोतारः वो युष्माकं श्रुताय स्तुतीनां श्रवणाय रक्षणाय च
> धृषच्छत्रून् धृषंतं सुशिप्रं सुहनुमिंद्रं प्रति हुवे । ह्वयामि । तूर्णाशं न
> यथा घर्मेऽभितस्तः पुमांस्तूर्णाशमुदकं । तथा च यास्कः । तूर्णाशमुदकं
> भवति तूर्णमश्नुते । नि. ५-१६ । इति । गिरेरधि मेघं प्रति ह्वयति । पर्वतो
> गिरिरिति मेघनामसु पाठात् । तद्वदित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O praisers, for the hearing of your praises and for your protection, I call upon Indra, who overcomes enemies and has a handsome jaw. He is called as a man tormented by the heat in the hot season calls for tūrṇāśa, water. Yāska says, in the Nirukta, that tūrṇāśa is water, because it quickly pervades (Ni. 5-16). He calls to the cloud from the mountain, since mountain and giri are read among the names of the cloud. The meaning is that he calls Indra in the same way.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O praisers) गिरेः अधि (*gireḥ adhi*)—from the cloud, as people tormented by the heat of the sun in summer
- शूर्णाशं न (*śūrṇāśaṃ na*)—desire water
- धृषत् (*dhṛṣat*)—the one who overcomes enemies
- सुशिप्रं (*suśipraṃ*)—Indra, who has an attractive cheek
- वः (*vaḥ*)—your
- श्रुताय (*śrutāya*)—for hearing the praises
- अवसे (*avase*)—and for protection
- प्रति हुवे (*prati huve*)—I pray and call

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O praisers, as people exhausted by the heat of the summer sun desire water from the cloud, I pray and call upon Indra, who overcomes enemies and has an attractive cheek, for your protection and for the hearing of your praises.

##### English translation
I invoke the victorious handsome-jawed Indra for your protection, and to hear (your praises), as (a traveller invokes) the water from the cloud.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

तूर्णाशं (*tūrṇāśaṃ*)—Tūrṇāśa means water. Yāska has explained this word: तूर्णाशं न यथा घर्मेऽभितस्तः पुमांस्तूर्णाशमुदकं । तथा च यास्कः । तूर्णाशमुदकं भवति तूर्णमश्नुते । नि. ५-१६ । इति । गिरेरधि मेघं प्रति ह्वयति । पर्वतो गिरिरिति मेघनामसु पाठात् । तद्वदित्यर्थः ॥ (ऎलै स्तोतृजनरे) गिरेः अधि—बेसिगॆयल्लि बिसिलिनिंद संतप्तराद जनरु मेघदिंद । शूर्णाशं न—उदकवन्नु बयसुवंतॆ । धृषत्—शत्रुधर्षकनू । सुशिप्रं—आकर्षक वाद कॆन्नॆयुळ्ळवनू आद इंद्रनन्नु । वः—निम्म । श्रुताय—स्तोत्रगळन्नु केळुवु दक्कागियू । अवसे—रक्षणार्थवागियू । प्रति हुवे—प्रार्थिसि करॆयुत्तेनॆ. ऎलै स्तोतृजनरे, बेसिगॆय बिसिलिनिंद बळलिद जनरु मेघदिंद उदकवन्नु अपेक्षिसु वंतॆ, शत्रुधर्षकनू, आकर्षकवाद कॆन्नॆयुळ्ळवनू आद इंद्रनन्नु रक्षणार्थवागियू निम्म स्तुति गळन्नु केळुवुदक्कागियू प्रार्थिसि करॆयुत्तेनॆ. I invoke the victorious handsome-jawed Indra for your protection, and to hear (your praises), as (a traveller invokes) the water from the cloud. तूर्णाशं—तूर्णाशवॆंदरॆ उदकवु. ई शब्दक्कॆ यास्करु अर्थविवरणॆमाडिद्दारॆ— तूर्णाशं । तूर्णाशमुदकं भवति तूर्णमश्नुते । तूर्णाशं न गिरेरधि इत्यपि निगमो भवति । (*tūrṇāśaṃ na yathā gharme 'bhitastaḥ pumāṃstūrṇāśamudakaṃ | tathā ca yāskaḥ | tūrṇāśamudakaṃ bhavati tūrṇamaśnute | ni. 5-16 | iti | gireradhi meghaṃ prati hvayati | parvato giririti meghanāmasu pāṭhāt | tadvadityarthaḥ ‖ (èlai stotṛjanare) gireḥ adhi—besigèyalli bisiliniṃda saṃtaptarāda janaru meghadiṃda | śūrṇāśaṃ na—udakavannu bayasuvaṃtè | dhṛṣat—śatrudharṣakanū | suśipraṃ—ākarṣaka vāda kènnèyuḷḷavanū āda iṃdranannu | vaḥ—nimma | śrutāya—stotragaḷannu keḷuvu dakkāgiyū | avase—rakṣaṇārthavāgiyū | prati huve—prārthisi karèyuttenè. èlai stotṛjanare, besigèya bisiliniṃda baḷalida janaru meghadiṃda udakavannu apekṣisu vaṃtè, śatrudharṣakanū, ākarṣakavāda kènnèyuḷḷavanū āda iṃdranannu rakṣaṇārthavāgiyū nimma stuti gaḷannu keḷuvudakkāgiyū prārthisi karèyuttenè. I invoke the victorious handsome-jawed Indra for your protection, and to hear (your praises), as (a traveller invokes) the water from the cloud. tūrṇāśaṃ—tūrṇāśavèṃdarè udakavu. ī śabdakkè yāskaru arthavivaraṇèmāḍiddārè— tūrṇāśaṃ | tūrṇāśamudakaṃ bhavati tūrṇamaśnute | tūrṇāśaṃ na gireradhi ityapi nigamo bhavati |*) (Ni. 5-16). Tūrṇāśa means water; this water flows swiftly, or quickly spreads and extends all around. Therefore tūrṇāśa means water. In this matter there is also the example of the Vedic sentence तूर्णाशं न गिरेरधि (*tūrṇāśaṃ na gireradhi*).

### Printed p. 231

तूर्णाशं (*tūrṇāśaṃ*)—water. Tūrṇaśa means water. For this word Yāska has given the explanation तूर्णाशमुदकं भवति तूर्णमश्नुते (*tūrṇāśamudakaṃ bhavati tūrṇamaśnute*) (Ni. 5-16). Tūrṇa means eating very quickly; that is, because water, unlike solid food, need not be chewed and eaten but can easily be drunk, water is named tūrṇaśa: this is the meaning.

#### Ṛk 5

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स गोरश्व॑स्य॒ वि व्र॒जं मं॑दा॒नः सो॒म्येभ्यः॑ ।  
> पुरं॒ न शू॑र दर्षसि ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । गोः । अश्वस्य । वि । व्रजं । मंदानः । सोम्येभ्यः ।  
> पुरं । न । शूर । दर्षसि ॥ ५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे शूरेंद्र स प्रसिद्धस्त्वं मंदानो मोदमानो गोरश्वस्य च व्रजं
> निवासस्थानं सोम्येभ्यः सोमार्हेभ्यः पुरं न शत्रूणां नगरमिव वि दर्षसि ।
> विवृतद्वारं करोषि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O hero Indra, you, the famous one, rejoicing, open for those who are worthy of soma the dwelling place of cattle and horses, the pen, as one lays open a city of enemies; that is, you make its door open.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शूर (*śūra*)—O valiant Indra
- सः (*saḥ*)—you, the famous one
- मंदानः (*maṃdānaḥ*)—delighted by our praises
- सोम्येभ्यः (*somyebhyaḥ*)—for us, who have prepared soma juice for you
- पुरं न (*puraṃ na*)—as one opens the gates of the towns of enemies
- गोः (*goḥ*)—of cows
- अश्वस्य (*aśvasya*)—and of horses
- व्रजं (*vrajaṃ*)—the door of the pen
- विदर्षसि (*vidarṣasi*)—keep open

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O valiant Indra, you who are pleased by our praises, open the door of the pen filled with cows and horses, as the gates of the towns of enemies are opened by force, in order to give to us, who have prepared soma juice for you.

##### English translation
Exhilarated by the Soma draughts, you, hero, throw open the pastures of the cattle and horses like a (hostile) city.

### Printed p. 232

#### Ṛk 6

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यदि॑ मे रा॒रणः॑ सु॒त उ॒क्थे वा॒ दध॑से॒ चनः॑ ।  
> आ॒रादुप॑ स्व॒धा ग॑हि ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यदि । मे । ररणः । सुते । उक्ते । वा । दधसे । चनः ।  
> आरात् । उप । स्वधा । आ । गहि ॥ ६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र मे मम सुतेऽभिषुते सोम उक्ते स्तोत्रे वा यदि रारणः
> रमसे चनोऽन्नं यदि च दधसे मह्यं प्रयच्छसि तर्ह्यारूद्दूरात् स्वधान्नेनोपा गहि । उपागच्छ ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, if you take delight in my pressed soma or in my praise, and if you give me food, then come near from afar with food of your own.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- (O Indra) मे (*me*)—pressed by me
- सुते (*sute*)—in the soma juice
- वा (*vā*)—or
- उक्ते (*ukte*)—in the praises that I recite
- यदि रराणिः (*yadi rarāṇiḥ*)—if you have found delight
- चनः (*canaḥ*)—food
- दधसे (*dadhase*)—if you give to me
- आरात् (*ārāt*)—even if you are in a distant region, from there
- स्वधा (*svadhā*)—together with the food that is to be given to us
- उप आ गहि (*upa ā gahi*)—graciously come near to us

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, if you find delight in the soma juice that I have pressed and prepared for you, or in the praises that I recite, and if you give me food, then, even if you are in a distant region, come near to us from there, bringing the food that is to be given to us.

##### English translation
If you are propitiated by my libation and praise. bestow food (upon me), come with viands from afar.

#### Ṛk 7

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> व॒यं घा॑ ते॒ अपि॑ ष्मसि स्तो॒तार॑ इंद्र गिर्वणः ।  
> त्वं नो॑ जिन्व सोमपाः ॥ ७ ॥

### Printed p. 234

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वयं । घ । ते । अपि । स्मसि । स्तोतारः । इंद्र । गिर्वणः ।  
> त्वं । नः । जिन्व । सोमपाः ॥ ७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे गिर्वणो गीर्भिर्वननीयेंद्र ते तवापि वयं घ वयं खलु स्तोतारः
> स्मसि । भवामः । हे सोमपाः सोमस्य पातरिंद्र त्वं नोऽस्माञ्जिन्व । प्रीणयसि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Girvaṇas, Indra, who are to be served with songs, we indeed are your praisers. O Somapa, Indra, drinker of soma, please us.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- गिर्वणः (*girvaṇaḥ*)—attracted by words of praise
- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- वयं घ (*vayaṃ gha*)—we also
- ते अपि (*te api*)—addressing you
- स्तोतारः (*stotāraḥ*)—as praisers
- स्मसि (*smasi*)—we are
- सोमपाः (*somapāḥ*)—O Indra, drinker of soma
- त्वं (*tvaṃ*)—you
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- जिन्व (*jinva*)—gladden

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, who are attracted by words of praise, we too praise you alone. O Indra, drinker of soma, gladden us.

##### English translation
Indra, you are pleased by praise, we are your adorers; do you therefore, who are the drinker of the Soma, be generous unto us.

#### Ṛk 8

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त नः॑ पि॒तुमा भ॑र संररा॒णो अवि॑क्षितं ।  
> मघ॑व॒न्भूरि॑ ते॒ वसु॑ ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । नः । पितुं । आ । भर । संरराणः । अविक्षितं ।  
> मघवन् । भूरि । ते । वसु ॥ ८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उतापि च हे मघवन् संरराणः संरममाणस्त्वमविक्षितमविक्षीणं पितुतुन्नं । सृक्षः पितुरित्यन्ननामसु पाठात् । नोऽस्मभ्यमा भर । अहर- । ते.शव वसुधनं भूर्यधिकं हि ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

And moreover, O Maghavan, you, delighting in us, bring to us undiminished food; pitu is read among the names of food. Your wealth is abundant, indeed greater. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—and
- हे मघवन् (*he maghavan*)—O wealthy Indra
- संरराणः (*saṃrarāṇaḥ*)—pleased by our praises
- अविक्षितं (*avikṣitaṃ*)—that does not perish
- पितुं (*pituṃ*)—food
- नः (*naḥ*)—to us
- आ भर (*ā bhara*)—bring
- ते (*te*)—your
- वसु (*vasu*)—wealth
- भूरि (*bhūri*)—is abundant

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Moreover, O wealthy Indra, you who are pleased by our praises, bring us food that does not perish. The wealth that you have is boundless.

##### English translation
Gratified by us, bring us undiminished food, Maghavan, for vast is your wealth.

#### Ṛk 9

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> उ॒त नो॒ गोम॑तस्कृधि॒ हिर॑ण्यवतो अ॒श्विनः॑ ।  
> इळा॑भिः॒ सं र॑भेमहि ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> उत । नः । गोऽमतः । कृधि । हिरण्यवतः । आश्विनः ।  
> इळाभिः । सं । रभेमहि ॥ ९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> उतापि च हे इंद्र नोऽस्मान् गोमतो गोमिनः कृधि । कुरु । अश्विनोऽश्वयुक्तान् कृधि । हिरण्यवतो धनवतश्च कृधि । इळाभिरन्नैश्च सं रभेमहि । वयं
> संरब्धा भवेम ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

And moreover, O Indra, make us possessors of cattle, possessors of horses, and possessors of gold, that is, of wealth. May we be well provided with iḷā, that is, with foods.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- उत (*uta*)—and, O Indra
- नः (*naḥ*)—us
- गोमतः (*gomataḥ*)—possessors of cattle
- अश्विनः (*aśvinaḥ*)—possessors of horses
- हिरण्यवतः (*hiraṇyavataḥ*)—possessors of wealth
- कृधि (*kṛdhi*)—make (and may we)
- इळाभिः (*iḷābhiḥ*)—with foods (obtain foods)
- सं रभेमहि (*saṃ rabhemahi*)—may we possess

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

And, O Indra, give us cattle and horses and make us possessors of wealth. And may we always have foods.

### Printed p. 235

##### English translation
Make us (Indra) possessed of cattle, of gold, and horses: may we prosper with abundant viands.

#### Ṛk 10

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> बृ॒बदु॑क्थं हवामहे सृ॒प्रक॑रस्नमू॒तये॑ ।  
> साधु॑ कृ॒ण्वंत॒मव॑से ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> बृबत् 5 उक्कं । हवामहे । सृप्रकरस्नं । ऊतये ।  
> साधु । कृण्वंतं । अवसे ॥ १० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऊतये लोकस्य रक्षणाय सृप्रकरस्नं प्रसृतबाहुं । करसौ बाहू कर्मणां
> प्रस्नातार्ः । नि. ६-१७ । इति यास्कवचनात् । अवसे लोकस्य पालनाय साधु
> कृण्वंतं साधु कुर्वंतं बृबदुक्थं महदुक्थमिंद्रं हवामहे । ह्ययामः ।
> तथा च यास्कः । बृबदुक्थो महदुक्थो वक्तव्यमस्मा उक्धं । नि. ६-४ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

For the protection of the world, Indra is the one whose arms are stretched out (सृप्रकरस्नं (*sṛprakarasnaṃ*)). Sāyaṇa supports this with Yāska's statement that the two arms are the ones that accomplish actions (Ni. 6-17). **NOTE** For the safeguarding of the world, we invoke Indra, who does good deeds and is of great praise (बृबदुक्थं (*bṛbadukthaṃ*) means one of great praise). Accordingly Yāska says that a great praise is to be spoken for him (Ni. 6-4).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऊतये (*ūtaye*)—for the protection of the world
- सृप्रकरस्नं (*sṛprakarasnaṃ*)—who holds up his arms
- अवसे (*avase*)—for protecting the world
- साधु (*sādhu*)—good deeds alone
- कृण्वंतं (*kṛṇvaṃtaṃ*)—who does
- बृबदुक्कं (*bṛbadukkaṃ*)—Indra, who is praised with great hymns
- हवामहे (*havāmahe*)—we pray and call

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

We pray to and call Indra, who for the protection of the world has lifted up and stretched out his arms, who performs excellent and manifold deeds of valour to save the people of the world from distress, and who is praised with great hymns.

##### English translation
We invoke Indra, who is greatly to be praised, whose arm is stretched out for the protection (of the world), acting nobly for our defence.

### Printed p. 236

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

While explaining the derivation of the word सृप्रः (*sṛpraḥ*), Yāska has also given the derivation of the word सृप्रकरस्नं (*sṛprakarasnaṃ*) that occurs in this ṛk.
सृप्रः सर्पणात् । इदमपीतरत् सृप्रमेतस्मादेव सर्पिर्वा तैलं वा । सृप्रकरस्नमूतये इत्यपि निगमो भवति । करस्य बाहू कर्मण ० प्रस्नातारौ ॥ (*sṛpraḥ sarpaṇāt | idamapītarat sṛprametasmādeva sarpirvā tailaṃ vā | sṛprakarasnamūtaye ityapi nigamo bhavati | karasya bāhū karmaṇa 0 prasnātārau ‖*) (Ni. 6-17)
That is: the word सृप्रः (*sṛpraḥ*) is derived from the root सृप् (*sṛp*), which means to slip away or glide. When the word सृप्र (*sṛpra*) means ghee or oil, it too is derived from this same root. सृप्रकरस्नमूतये (*sṛprakarasnamūtaye*) is an example from the Veda. करस्य (*karasya*) means the two stretched-out hands. In the present ṛk the meaning is that Indra has raised and stretched out his two hands in order to protect the people.
बृबदुक्कं (*bṛbadukkaṃ*): to explain this word also Yāska has given its derivation:
बृबदुक्कं । बृबदुक्को महदुक्को वक्तव्यमस्मा उक्षमिति बृबदुक्को वा । बृबदुक्कं हवामहे इत्यपि निगमो भवति ॥ (*bṛbadukkaṃ | bṛbadukko mahadukko vaktavyamasmā ukṣamiti bṛbadukko vā | bṛbadukkaṃ havāmahe ityapi nigamo bhavati ‖*) (Ni. 6-4)
बृबदुक्क (*bṛbadukka*) means a great hymn composed in an excellent manner. The meaning of the word is: Indra, who is praised with such excellent praises. Taking the very sentence बृबदुक्कं हवामहे (*bṛbadukkaṃ havāmahe*) from the present ṛk as the example, he has explained the meaning.

#### Ṛk 11

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यः सं॒स्थॆ चि॑च्छ॒तक्र॑तु॒रादीं॑ कृ॒णोति॑ वृत्र॒हा ।  
> ज॒रि॒तृभ्यः॑ पुरू॒वसु॑ ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । संस्थे । चित् । शतक्रतुः । आत् । ईं । कृणोति । वृत्रहा ।  
> जरितृभ्यः । पुरुषसुः ॥ ११ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यः प्रसिद्ध इंद्रः संस्थॆ संग्रामे शतुक्रतुर्बहुकर्मा भवति अपि चादनंतरमीमिदं शत्रुवधादिकं कृणोति करोति चिदेव आयमिंद्रो वृत्रहा शत्रूणां

### Printed p. 237

> हंता भवति । किंच जरितृभ्यः स्तोतृणामर्थे पुरूवसुर्बहुधनो भवति । न
> स्वार्थमित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The well-known Indra becomes a doer of many deeds in battle, and thereafter he performs this slaying of enemies and the like. This Indra, the slayer of Vṛtra, is the killer of enemies. Further, for the sake of the praisers he is rich in much wealth, that is, not for his own sake.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—the well-known Indra who
- संस्थॆ (*saṃsthè*)—in battle
- शतक्रतुः (*śatakratuḥ*)—does many deeds of valour
- आत् (*āt*)—thereafter
- ईं (*īṃ*)—the deeds of slaying enemies and the like
- कृणोति (*kṛṇoti*)—does
- चित् (*cit*)—that same Indra
- वृत्रहा (*vṛtrahā*)—destroys enemies, and also
- जरितृभ्यः (*jaritṛbhyaḥ*)—to give to the people who praise him
- पुरुवसुः (*puruvasuḥ*)—possesses much wealth

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The well-known Indra who, performing many deeds of valour in battle, destroys enemies, that same Indra kills the enemies. In order to give to the people who praise him, Indra has much wealth with him.

##### English translation
The slayer of Vritra, the occomplisher of a hundred exploits in war, achieves them that he may be the giver of much wealth to his worshippers.

#### Ṛk 12

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स नः॑ श॒क्रश्चि॒दा श॑क॒द्दान॑वा अंतराभ॒रः ।  
> इंद्रो॒ विश्वा॑भिरू॒तिभिः॑ ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सः । नः । शक्रः । चित् । आ । शकत् । दानवान् । अंतरआभरः ।  
> इंद्रः । विश्वाभिः । ऊतिभिः ॥ १२ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> शक्रः शक्तः स इंद्रो नश्चिदस्मानप्या शकत् । शक्तान् करोतु । अपि चेंद्रो
> दानवान् विश्वाभिः सर्वैरूतिभिः पालनैरंतराभरोऽंतराहरश्चित्राणामापूरकः ।
> छिद्रापिधायीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

May that capable Indra make us also capable. And Indra, with all protections, that is, with all guardings, is the one who fills up the gaps. **NOTE** That is, he is the one who closes openings.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- शक्रः (*śakraḥ*)—the capable
- सः (*saḥ*)—that Indra
- नः चित् (*naḥ cit*)—us also
- आ शकत् (*ā śakat*)—may he make capable, and
- दानवान् (*dānavān*)—who gives liberally
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—Indra
- विश्वाभिः (*viśvābhiḥ*)—all

### Printed p. 238

- ऊतिभिः (*ūtibhiḥ*)—with his protections, our
- अंतराभरः (*aṃtarābharaḥ*)—will protect the regions that are exposed to danger

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May the capable Indra make us also powerful. May that Indra, who is of a liberal, giving nature, with all his protections guard us, who are exposed to danger.

##### English translation
May Sakra give us strength, may the liberal Indra with all protections be the supplier of our deficiencies.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

अंतराभरः—अंतराहरश्चित्राणामापूरकः (*aṃtarābharaḥ—aṃtarāharaścitrāṇāmāpūrakaḥ*) ।  When fighting, the enemies rush in at whatever place they find an opening. Not every region is impregnable. The same is true of soldiers. Even though they wear armour and the like to protect the body, there will always remain some part or limb that is exposed to danger. The idea suggested here is that Indra should protect, by his own power and capacity, such parts that may be exposed to danger (vulnerable points).

#### Ṛk 13

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यो रा॒यो॒३॒॑ ३ वनि॑र्म॒हान्तु॑सपा॒सः सु॑न्व॒तः सखा॑ ।  
> तमिंद्र॑म॒भि गा॑यत ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । रायः ! अवनिः । महान् । सुपारः । सुन्वतः । सखा ।  
> तं । इंद्रं । अभि । गायत ॥ १३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> य इंद्रो रायो धनस्यावनिः पालको महान् सर्वोत्तमः सुपारः
> शोभनपारणश्च भवति । यश्च सुन्वतः सोमाभिषवं कुर्वतो यजमानस्य सखा
> प्रियो भवति । तमिंद्रमभि गायत । अभिष्टुत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The Indra who is the protector of wealth, great, that is, the best of all, and a good ferryman across, and who is the friend of the sacrificer who presses the Soma, that Indra, sing to, that is, praise.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—the Indra who
- रायः (*rāyaḥ*)—for wealth
- अवनिः (*avaniḥ*)—is the protector
- महान् (*mahān*)—the best of all
- सुपारः (*supāraḥ*)—who is easily approached by those who praise him, and who is

### Printed p. 239

- सुन्वतः (*sunvataḥ*)—of the sacrificer who performs the pressing of Soma
- सखा (*sakhā*)—who is a dear friend
- तं इंद्रं (*taṃ iṃdraṃ*)—that same Indra
- अभि गायत (*abhi gāyata*)—praise with songs and the like **NOTE**

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

The Indra who is the protector of wealth, who is the best of all, who is easily accessible to those who praise him, who is a friend to the sacrificer who presses the Soma and performs the sacrifice, sing the praise of that very Indra and extol him.

##### English translation
Glorify that Indra who is the preserver of riches, the mighty, the conveyer beyond (calamity), the friend of offerer of the libation.

#### Ṛk 14

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> आ॒यं॒तारं॒ महि॑ स्थि॒रं पृत॑नासु श्रवो॒जितं॑ ।  
> भूरे॒रीशा॑न॒मोज॑सा ॥ १४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> आयंतारं । महि । स्थिरं । पृतनासु । श्रवःजितं ।  
> भूरेः । ईशानं । ओजसा ॥ १४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> आयंतारमागंतारं महि महांतं पृतनासु संग्रामेषु स्थिरमचलं
> श्रवोजितं श्रवसो जीतारमोजसा बलेन भूरेर्बहोर्धनस्येशानमीश्वरमभिगायत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Sing to Indra, who is the one who comes, great, firm and immovable in battles, the conqueror of fame, and by his strength the lord of much wealth.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- आयंकारं (*āyaṃkāraṃ*)—who comes near those who praise him
- महि (*mahi*)—of great glory
- पृथनासु (*pṛthanāsu*)—in battles
- स्थिरं (*sthiraṃ*)—who stands firm against the enemy without fleeing
- श्रवोजितं (*śravojitaṃ*)—who wins the food of the enemies, or who wins fame
- ओजसा (*ojasā*)—by his own power
- भूरेः (*bhūreḥ*)—of abundant wealth
- ईशानं (*īśānaṃ*)—and the lord (praise Indra)

### Printed p. 240

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Praise Indra, who comes near to those who praise him in order to favour them, who is of great glory, who in battles does not lose and flee but stands firm against the enemies, who seizes food from the enemies in battle or wins fame, and who by his own power is lord of an immeasurable mass of wealth.

##### English translation
Him who comes nigh, the mighty, the firm in battles, the acquirer of fame, the lord of the vast riches through his prowess.

#### Ṛk 15

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> नकिरस्य शचीनां नियंता सून्मत्तानां ।  
> नकि॑र्व॒क्ता न दा॒दिति॑ ॥ १५ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> नकिः । अस्य । शचीनां । नियंता । सूनृतानां ।  
> नकिः । वक्ता । न । दात् । इति ॥ १५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अस्येंद्रस्य सूनृतानां शोभनानां शचीनां कर्मणां । धीः शचीति कर्मनामसु पाठात् । नकिर्न कश्चिन्नियंता नियामकः । अयमिंद्रो न दादिति न
> प्रयच्छतीति नकिर्वक्ता न कश्चिद्वदति । किंतु सर्वोऽपि जनोऽयं प्रदातेत्येव
> ब्रवीतीत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Of this Indra, of the good deeds called शचीनां (*śacīnāṃ*), that is, of actions, for it is read so among the names of action, there is no controller, no regulator. And no one says that this Indra does not give, that he does not bestow; rather, everyone says only that he is a giver.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अस्य (*asya*)—of this Indra
- सूनृतानां (*sūnṛtānāṃ*)—of the excellent or auspicious
- शचीनां (*śacīnāṃ*)—for the deeds
- नियंता (*niyaṃtā*)—one who controls
- नकिः (*nakiḥ*)—there is no one
- न दात् इति (*na dāt iti*)—(and that this Indra) gives nothing to the people who praise him
- नकिः वक्ता (*nakiḥ vaktā*)—there is no one who says so

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

There is no one who controls Indra, saying that he should perform his excellent deeds only so far. Indra is not subject to anyone. And there is no man who says that this Indra gives nothing to the people who praise him. That is, everyone says that Indra gives those who praise him whatever they need.

### Printed p. 241

##### English translation
No one puts a limit to his glorious deeds, no one asserts that he is not generous.

#### Ṛk 16

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> न नू॒नं ब्र॒ह्मणा॑मृ॒णं प्रा॑शू॒नाम॑स्ति सुन्व॒तां ।  
> न सोमो॑ अप्र॒ता स॑पेः ॥ १६ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> न । नूनं । ब्रह्मणां । ऋणं । प्राशूनां । अस्ति । सुन्नतां ।  
> न । सोमः । अप्रका । पपे ॥ १६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> प्राशूनां । ये सोमं प्राश्नुवंति ते प्राशवः । तेषां सोमं सुन्वतां
> ब्रह्मणां ब्राह्मणानामृणं देवर्णं न नूनमस्ति । न खलु विद्यते । तथा च
> श्रूयते । एष वा अनृणो यः पुत्री यज्ञा ब्रह्मचारिवासी । त्रै. सं. ६-३-१०-५ ।
> इति । किंचाप्रतापिस्तीर्णधनेन सोमो न पपे । न पीयते । प्रभूतधनेनैव
> सोमः पीयत इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Those who partake of Soma are called प्राशूनां (*prāśūnāṃ*). Of those brahmans who press Soma there is certainly no debt to the gods, no such debt is found. So it is heard: "He alone is free of debt who has a son, has sacrificed and lives as a student of the Veda" (Tai. Saṃ. 6-3-10-5). Further, the Soma is not drunk by one whose wealth is not abundant; the meaning is that Soma is drunk only with abundant wealth. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- प्रारूनां (*prārūnāṃ*)—for the people who drink Soma
- सुन्वतां (*sunvatāṃ*)—who perform the Soma pressing in the sacrifice
- ब्रह्मणां (*brahmaṇāṃ*)—for the brahmans
- ऋणं (*ṛṇaṃ*)—the debt to the gods, which has to be repaid to the gods
- नूनं (*nūnaṃ*)—certainly
- न अस्ति (*na asti*)—there is not (and)
- अप्रता (*apratā*)—by those who have not abundant wealth
- सोमः (*somaḥ*)—the Soma juice
- न पपे (*na pape*)—is not drunk

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

For the people who drink Soma at sacrifices and the like, and who in performing a sacrifice crush the Soma plants, extract the juice, offer it to the gods according to rule in order to present it to them, and drink it themselves, there is no debt connected with the gods. And by those who lack sufficient wealth to carry out sacrifices and the like, the drinking of Soma is not possible.

### Printed p. 242

##### English translation
Verily no debt is due (to the gods) by those Soma-partaking Brahmans offering libations : the Soma is not drunk without the expenditure of boundless (wealth).

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this ṛk, Soma drinking and Soma pressing mean the drinking of Soma performed in the proper manner in the course of the sacrificial rites. For those who drink Soma in this way, following the performance of a sacrifice, there is no debt to the gods. And since persons of small wealth cannot perform a sacrifice, it is said here that such people cannot drink Soma. It is necessary here to explain what the debt to the gods is. On this subject the Taittirīya Śruti says as follows.
जायमानो वै ब्राह्मणस्त्रिभिर्यणवा जायते ब्रह्मचर्येणर्षिभ्यो यज्ञेन देवेभ्यः प्रजया पितृभ्य एष वा अनृणो यः पुत्री यज्वा ब्रह्मचारिवासी ॥ (*jāyamāno vai brāhmaṇastribhiryaṇavā jāyate brahmacaryeṇarṣibhyo yajñena devebhyaḥ prajayā pitṛbhya eṣa vā anṛṇo yaḥ putrī yajvā brahmacārivāsī ‖*) (Tai. Saṃ. 6-3-10-5)
That is, a brahman is born carrying three kinds of debts (loans); one who is born a brahman must repay the three debts called the debt to the sages, the debt to the gods and the debt to the ancestors. Otherwise he has no good destiny. The manner of repaying these debts is this. If, immediately after the initiation, he studies the Veda in the stage of studentship, serving his teacher, he repays the debt to the sages. Then, marrying at the proper time and becoming a householder, if he performs sacrifices and the like in order to worship the gods, he repays the debt to the gods. And by begetting a son on his wedded wife he repays the debt to the ancestors. Therefore the chief meaning of this Vedic sentence is that a brahman, for the discharge of these three kinds of debt, should after the initiation live in the teacher's house, complete the study of the Veda, marry with the teacher's permission, perform sacrifices and so satisfy the gods, beget a son at the proper time and keep doing good deeds. In the present ṛk only the matter of the debt to the gods is indicated. By the words Soma pressing, Soma drinking and the like, the performance of sacrifice is indicated. Since the proper drinking of Soma is prescribed only in sacrifices, the words Soma pressing and Soma drinking mean the performance of sacrifice. The chief meaning of the ṛk is that by doing these the debt to the gods is repaid.

### Printed p. 243

#### Ṛk 17

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पन्य॒ इदुप॑ गायत॒ पन्य॑ उ॒क्कानि॑ शंसत ।  
> ब्रह्मा॑ कृणोत॒ पन्य॒ इत् ॥ १७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पन्ये । इत् । उप । गायत । पन्ये । उक्तानि । शंसत ।  
> ब्रह्म । कृणोत । पन्ये । इत् ॥ १७ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे उपागातारः पन्य इत् स्तुत्य एवेंद्र उप गायत । उपगानं:कुरुत ।
> किंच पन्य एवेंद्र उक्तानि स्तोत्राणि शंसत । हे स्तोतार इति शेषः । पन्य इत्
> स्तुत्य एवेंद्रे ब्रह्माण्यन्यानि स्तोत्राणि कृणोत । कुरुत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O singers who sing near, sing to Indra, the praiseworthy alone; perform the singing near. Further, recite hymns of praise to Indra, the praiseworthy alone; the words "O praisers" are to be supplied. To Indra, the praiseworthy alone, offer other hymns of praise as well; make them.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पन्ये इत् (*panye it*)—(O singers of the Sāman, O attendant singers) in honour of Indra the praiseworthy alone
- उप गायत (*upa gāyata*)—sing with Sāman hymns (and)
- पन्ये (*panye*)—in honour of Indra the praiseworthy
- उक्तानि (*uktāni*)—hymns of praise
- शंसत (*śaṃsata*)—recite (O praisers)
- पन्ये इत् (*panye it*)—in honour of Indra the praiseworthy
- ब्रह्म (*brahma*)—hymns of praise
- कृणोत (*kṛṇota*)—compose and offer

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O singers of the Sāman, in honour of the praiseworthy Indra sing his praises with Sāman hymns. In honour of that praiseworthy Indra recite hymns of praise too. O praisers, in honour of the praiseworthy Indra compose and offer excellent praises.

##### English translation
Sing praises to the adorable (Indra), repeat prayers to the adorable (Indra), address hymns to the adorable (Indra).

#### Ṛk 18

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पन्य॒ आ द॑र्दिरच्छ॒ता स॒हस्रा॑ वा॒ज्यवृ॑तः ।  
> इंद्रो॒ यो यज्ज॑नो वृ॒धः ॥ १८ ॥

### Printed p. 244

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पन्यः । आ । दर्दिरत् । शता । सहस्रा । वाजी । अवृतः ।  
> इंद्रः । यः । यज्वनः । वृधः ॥ १८ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> यो वाजी बलवाञ्छता वीराणां शतानि सहस्रा सहस्राणि चा दर्दिरत्
> आभिमुख्येन दारयति सोऽयमिंद्रः शत्रुभिरवृतः पन्यः स्तुत्यो भवति । यज्जनो
> विधिनेष्टवतो यजमानस्य वृधो वर्धयिता च भवति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The powerful one who tears apart, face to face, hundreds and thousands of heroes, this Indra, unenclosed by enemies, is praiseworthy. He is the one who, for the sacrificer who has sacrificed according to rule, is the enhancer.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—the Indra who
- वाजी (*vājī*)—is mighty
- शता (*śatā*)—hundreds of heroes
- सहस्रा (*sahasrā*)—thousands of warriors
- आ दर्दिरत् (*ā dardirat*)—can easily tear apart in battle
- इंद्रः (*iṃdraḥ*)—such an Indra, is
- अवृतः (*avṛtaḥ*)—unconquerable by enemies
- पन्यः (*panyaḥ*)—praiseworthy
- यज्जनः (*yajjanaḥ*)—worshipped according to rule by the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice, and
- वृधः (*vṛdhaḥ*)—makes the sacrificer prosper

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra is exceedingly strong. In battle, without being surrounded by enemies, he alone can easily tear apart hundreds and thousands of heroes. This praiseworthy Indra, praised by the sacrificer who performs the sacrifice, brings him prosperity.

##### English translation
The powerful Indra, the discomforter of hundreds and thousands, unchecked (by foes), is adorable; he who is the benefactor of the sacrificer.

#### Ṛk 19

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> वि शू च॑र स्व॒धा अनु॑ कृष्णे॒नामन्वा॒हुवः॑ ।  
> इंद्र॒ पिब॑ सु॒तानां॑ ॥ १९ ॥

### Printed p. 245

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वि । सु । चर । स्वधाः । अनु । कृष्णीनां । अनु । आहुवः ।  
> इंद्र । पिब । सुतानां ॥ १९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र आहुव आह्वातव्यस्त्वं कृष्णीनां मनुष्याणां स्वधा हवींष्यनु सु
> सुष्टु वि चर । द्वितीयोऽनु पूरणः । सुतानामभिषुतान् सोमांश्च पिब ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, you who are to be invoked, move well about the oblations (स्वधा (*svadhā*)) of men, who are called कृष्णीनां (*kṛṣṇīnāṃ*). The second अनु (*anu*) only completes the phrase. And drink the Soma juices that have been pressed.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- आहुवः (*āhuvaḥ*)—you who are invoked
- कृष्णीनां (*kṛṣṇīnāṃ*)—offered by men
- स्वधाति अनु (*svadhāti anu*)—to the vicinity of the oblation
- सु वि चर (*su vi cara*)—you go, do you not
- सुतानां (*sutānāṃ*)—the Soma juices pressed and made ready
- पिब (*piba*)—drink

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you who are invoked at sacrifices and the like, go to the oblation offered by men in order to partake of it. O Indra, drink the Soma juices that have been pressed and made ready.

##### English translation
Indra, you are to be invoked of men: come to their offered viands, drink of (their) libations.

#### Ṛk 20

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पिब॒ स्वधै॑नवानामु॒त यस्तुग्रे॒ सचा॑ ।  
> उ॒तायमिं॑द्र॒ यस्तव॑ ॥ २० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पिब । स्वधैनवानां । उत । यः । तुग्रै । सचा ।  
> उत । अयं । इंद्र । यः । तव ॥ २० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> : रामचंद्रन कीर्ति ।
> हे इंद्र स्वधैनवानां स्वधैनवान् स्वभूतपयसो धेनोः संबंधिनः
> सोमान् । धेन्वा क्रीतानित्यर्थः । तथा च श्रूयते । धेन्वा क्रीणातीति । उतापि च

### Printed p. 246

> यः सोमस्तुग्र उदके । बुसं तुग्रमित्युदकनामसु पाठात् । सचा संसृष्टः
> तमपि सोमं पिब । उतापि च यः सोमस्तव त्वदीयस्त्वामुद्दिश्य गृहीतः
> सोऽयं त्वया पातव्य इति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, drink the Soma belonging to the cow that gives her own milk, that is, Soma bought with a cow. So it is heard: "He buys with a cow." Moreover, drink also the Soma that is in water, mixed with it; water is read among the names of water in the list of synonyms. And the Soma that is yours, taken for you, is to be drunk by you; this last is the word to be supplied.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- स्वधैनवानां (*svadhainavānāṃ*)—the Soma that has been bought for you by giving cows
- उत (*uta*)—and
- यः (*yaḥ*)—the Soma which
- तुग्रे सचा (*tugre sacā*)—is mixed with water
- यः (*yaḥ*)—the Soma which
- तव (*tava*)—has been pressed for you alone
- अयं (*ayaṃ*)—such Soma as this, may it be drunk by you

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, for your drinking, these Soma plants have been bought by giving cows, well pressed, mixed with water and made ready for drinking. Drink such Soma.

##### English translation
Drink of the (Soma) purchased by the milch cow, that (Soma) which is mixed with water, that which, Ida is especially yours.

##### विशेषविषयगळु (Special Topics)

In this ṛk it is said that the Soma has been bought by giving cows. Since the Soma plants grow on some river banks and in high mountain regions, not everyone can easily fetch them. Therefore it has become the custom to buy them, at great cost, from those who have brought them from distant regions, and to use them in sacrifices and the like. This buying is called Soma purchase. It is considered a rite forming part of the sacrifice. When Soma plants are bought, it is the custom to give many kinds of things in exchange. The sentence of the Taittirīya Saṃhitā tells clearly what those things are.
अजया क्रीणाति सतपसमेवैनं क्रीणाति हिरण्येन क्रीणाति सशुक्रमेष्टैनं क्रीणाति धेन्वा क्रीणाति साशिरमेष्टैनं क्रीणात्कृषभेण क्रीणाति सेंद्रमेवैनं क्रीणात्यनडुहा क्रीणाति वह्निर्वा अनड्वान् वह्निनैव वह्नि यज्ञस्य क्रीणाति मिथुनाभ्यां क्रीणाति मिथुनसा वरुध्यै वाससा क्रीणाति सर्वदेवत्यं वै वासः सर्वाभ्य एवैनं देवताभ्यः क्रीणाति दश संपद्यंते ॥ (*ajayā krīṇāti satapasamevainaṃ krīṇāti hiraṇyena krīṇāti saśukrameṣṭainaṃ krīṇāti dhenvā krīṇāti sāśirameṣṭainaṃ krīṇātkṛṣabheṇa krīṇāti seṃdramevainaṃ krīṇātyanaḍuhā krīṇāti vahnirvā anaḍvān vahninaiva vahni yajñasya krīṇāti mithunābhyāṃ krīṇāti mithunasā varudhyai vāsasā krīṇāti sarvadevatyaṃ vai vāsaḥ sarvābhya evainaṃ devatābhyaḥ krīṇāti daśa saṃpadyaṃte ‖*) (Kṛ. Saṃ. 6 1-10-1, 2) **NOTE**

### Printed p. 247

It is said that one may buy by giving any one of ten kinds of goods: goat, austerity, gold, śukra, a milch cow, milk, a bull, an ox, a pair, a garment. Among these, it is said in the present ṛk that the Soma bought by giving a dhenu, that is, a cow that gives milk, has been made ready for Indra.

#### Ṛk 21

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अती॑हि मन्युषा॒विणं॑ सुषु॒नांस॑मु॒पार॑णे ।  
> इ॒मं रा॒तं सु॒तं पि॑ब ॥ २१ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अति । इहि । मन्युसाविनं । सुसुवांसं । उपअरणे ।  
> इमं । रातं । सुतं । पिब ॥ २१ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र मन्युषाविणं क्रोधेन सोमं सुन्वंतमतीहि । अतिगच्छः । तथोपारणे । ब्राह्मणा उपेत्य यस्मिन्नेशे न रमंते स उपारणः । तस्मिन्नेशे सुषुवांसं सुन्वंतमतीहि । इमं रातं ब्राह्मणोपद्रवरहिते देशेऽस्माभिर्दत्तमिमं
> सुतं सोमं पिब ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, pass beyond the one who presses Soma in anger. Likewise, in a place called उपारणे (*upāraṇe*), where brahmans, having come, do not delight, pass beyond one who presses Soma. Drink this Soma that has been given, that is, pressed and offered by us in a place free from the disturbance of brahmans.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मन्युषाविणं (*manyuṣāviṇaṃ*)—(O Indra) for the Soma drinking prepared by one who presses Soma in a fit of anger
- अति इहि (*ati ihi*)—do not go
- उपारणे (*upāraṇe*)—in a place without brahmans
- सुषुवांसं (*suṣuvāṃsaṃ*)—for the Soma drinking prepared by one who presses, do not go either (but)
- इमं रातं (*imaṃ rātaṃ*)—prepared by us in this place free of disturbance by brahmans
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—this Soma juice
- पिब (*piba*)—drink

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, do not go there to drink the Soma juice pressed by one who is always filled with anger. And do not drink the Soma pressed in a place without brahmans, that is, in a place where no sacrifice is performed. But this Soma that we have prepared has been pressed in a good place by brahmans, hallowed with mantras, and made ready. Drink such Soma juice.

### Printed p. 248

##### English translation
Indra, pass by the man who offers the libation in anger, him who pours it out upon a spot disapproved of ; drink this presented Soma.

#### Ṛk 22

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒हि ति॒स्रः प॑रा॒वत॑ इ॒हि पंच॒ जना॒ अति॑ ।  
> धेना॑ इंद्राव॒चाक॑शत् ॥ २२ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इहि । तिस्रः । परावतः । इहि । पंच । जनान् । अति ।  
> धेनाः । इंद्र । अव्यचाकशत् ॥ २२ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र धेना अस्मदीयाः स्तुतीरवचाकशद्योऽपश्यत् स त्वं पारावतो
> दूरात् । आरे परावत इति दूरनामसु पाठात् । तिस्रोऽग्रपृष्ठ पार्श्वदिश इहि ।
> गच्छ । अनेनाग्रतः पृष्ठतः पार्श्वतश्चेंद्रस्यागमनमाशास्त्रे । अपि च पंच जनान्
> मनुष्यानतीहि । अतिगच्छ । यद्वा। गंधर्वाः पितरो देवा असुरा रक्षांसि च पंच
> जनाः । तानतीहीत्यर्थः । तथा च यास्कः । गंधर्वाः पितरो देवा असुरा रक्षांसीत्येके चत्वारो वर्णा निषादः पंचम इत्यौपमन्यवः । नि. ३-८ । इति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, you who have seen our praises (धेना (*dhenā*) means our praises), come from far away; "far" because परावत (*parāvata*) is read among the names of distance. Come from the three directions, front, back and side; by this the coming of Indra from in front, from behind and from the side is wished for. Further, pass beyond the five peoples, that is, men. Or else: Gandharvas, fathers, gods, asuras and rākṣasas are the five peoples; pass beyond them. So Yāska says: "Gandharvas, fathers, gods, asuras and rākṣasas, say some; the four classes and the Niṣāda as the fifth, say the followers of Upamanyu" (Ni. 3-8).

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इंद्र (*iṃdra*)—O Indra
- धेनाः (*dhenāḥ*)—our praises
- अवचाकशत् (*avacākaśat*)—you who see and know
- परावतः (*parāvataḥ*)—from a distant region
- तिस्रः (*tisraḥ*)—from three sides, that is, from the front, the back and the sides
- इहि (*ihi*)—come here
- पंच जनान् (*paṃca janān*)—the five peoples, Gandharvas and the rest
- अति (*ati*)—passing beyond
- इहि (*ihi*)—come here

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, you who see and know from afar the words of praise we make, come to us from the distant region, from the front, from behind or from the sides. Or, disregarding the five peoples beginning with the Gandharvas, or the people of five tribes, who are on the way, pass beyond them and come to us.

### Printed p. 249

##### English translation
Indra, you have beheld our praises; proceed in three directions from a distance. pass beyond the five orders of beings.

#### Ṛk 23

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> सूर्यो॑ र॒त्मिं यथा॑ सृ॒जा त्वा॑ यच्छंतु मे॒ गिरः॑ ।  
> नि॒म्म मापो॒ न स॒ध्र्य॑क् ॥ २३ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> सूर्यः । रश्मिं । यथा । सृज । आ । त्वा । यच्छंतु । मे । गिरः ।  
> निम्मं । आपः । न । सध्र्यक् ॥ २३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे इंद्र सूर्यो यथा रश्मिं रश्मीन्किरणानश्वप्रग्रहान्वा विसृजति तथा मह्यं
> धनं विसृज । अपि च मे मदीया गिरः स्तुतयः सध्यक् सह त्वामा यच्छंतु ।
> निम्ममापो न । यथा निम्मदेशमापः सह परिगृह्णंति तद्वदित्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, as the sun lets go his rays (that is, his beams, or else the reins of his horses), so let go wealth for me. And may my praises together reach you; as the waters together take hold of a low place, so, is the meaning.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- सूर्यः (*sūryaḥ*)—(O Indra) the sun
- यथा (*yathā*)—in the way in which
- रश्मिं (*raśmiṃ*)—spreads his rays all around, like that
- सृज (*sṛja*)—give me wealth and the like
- निम्मं (*nimmaṃ*)—in a low region
- आपो न (*āpo na*)—as water flows and comes to rest
- मे (*me*)—my
- गिरः (*giraḥ*)—words of praise
- सध्र्यक् (*sadhryak*)—joining together as one
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- यच्छंतु (*yacchaṃtu*)—may they reach

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, as the sun spreads his rays around himself, make wealth and the like come and gather near me. As flowing water goes to a low place and comes to rest, may the words of praise that I make go to you and join you.

##### English translation
As the sun disperses his rays, so you disperse (wealth upon me):-may my praises rapidly draw you, as waters (reach) the low ground.

### Printed p. 250

#### Ṛk 24

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अध्व॑र्य॒वा तु हि षिं॒च सोमं॑ वी॒राय॑ शि॒प्रिणे॑ ।  
> भरा॑ सु॒तस्य॑ पी॒तये॑ ॥ २४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अध्वयो इति । आ । तु । हि । सिंच । सोमं । वीराय । शिप्रिणे ।  
> भर । सुतस्य । पीतये ॥ २४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे अध्वर्यवः शिप्रिणे हनूमते वीराय शूरायेंद्राय सोमं तु
> हि क्षिप्रमेवा सिंच । सुतस्य सुतं सोमं पीतये पानाय च भर । आहर च ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O adhvaryus, to Indra the hero, the brave one, who is शिप्रिणे (*śipriṇe*) (possessed of jaws), pour the Soma quickly. And bring the pressed Soma for his drinking.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अध्वयो (*adhvayo*)—O adhvaryu
- शिप्रिणे (*śipriṇe*)—shining with attractive cheeks
- वीराय (*vīrāya*)—for the brave Indra
- सोमं तु (*somaṃ tu*)—the Soma juice
- हि (*hi*)—quickly
- आ सिंच (*ā siṃca*)—press and make ready
- सुतस्य (*sutasya*)—the pressed Soma juice
- पीतये (*pītaye*)—for Indra's drinking
- भर (*bhara*)—bring

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O adhvaryu, quickly make ready the Soma juice for the brave Indra, who shines with attractive cheeks. And bring that pressed Soma juice for the drinking of Indra.

##### English translation
Adhwaryu, quickly pour forth the soma to the hero Indra with the goodly jaws; bring the Soma for his drinking.

#### Ṛk 25

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> य उ॒द्दः फ॑लि॒गं भि॒नन्नृ १ १॒॑क्सिंधू॑र॒वासृ॑जत् ।  
> यो गोषु॑ प॒क्वं धा॒रय॑त् ॥ २५ ॥

### Printed p. 251

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । उद्द्नः । फलिगं । भिनत् । न्यक् । सिंधून् । अव्यअसृजत् ।  
> यः । गोषु । पक्वं । धारयत् । २५ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे उदन्' उदकार्थं फलिगं मेघं । रैवतः फलिग इति मेघनामसु
> पाठात् । भिनत् अभिनत् । सिंधूनपश्चांतरिक्षान्यगर्वागवासृजत् । यश्च गोषु
> पक्वं पयो धारयत् आधारयत् । स इंद्र इत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

He who, for the sake of water, split the cloud; फलिग (*phaliga*) is read among the names of cloud, the passage being "raivata is phaliga". He let the waters of the atmosphere, called सिंधून् (*siṃdhūn*), flow down; and he who has placed ripe milk in the cows: that is Indra. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- यः (*yaḥ*)—the Indra who, for the sake of water
- फलिगं (*phaligaṃ*)—the cloud
- भिनत् (*bhinat*)—split
- सिंधून् (*siṃdhūn*)—the waters that were in the cloud
- न्यत् (*nyat*)—downward from the atmosphere
- अवासृजत् (*avāsṛjat*)—made pour
- यः (*yaḥ*)—the Indra who
- गोषु (*goṣu*)—in the cows
- पक्वं (*pakvaṃ*)—the ripe milk
- धारयत् (*dhārayat*)—placed; for the drinking of such an Indra, bring the Soma juice

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

For the drinking of Indra of such greatness, who split the cloud in order to pour down rain, who made the water that was in the atmosphere pour down as rain, and who established milk in the cows, O adhvaryu, bring the Soma juice.

##### English translation
Who clove the cloud for (the issue of) the rain, who sent down the waters, who placed the mature (milk) in thc cattle.

#### Ṛk 26

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अह॑न्व॒त्रमृचे॑षम और्णवा॒भम॑ही॒शुवं॑ ।  
> हि॒मेना॑विध्य॒दर्बु॑दं ॥ २६ ॥

### Printed p. 252

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अहन् । वृत्रं । यजीषमः । और्णवाभं । अहीशुवं ।  
> हिमेन । अविध्यत् । अर्बुदं ॥ २६ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> ऋचीषम ऋचा दीप्त्या सम इंद्रो वृत्रं वृत्रनामकं शत्रुमहन् ।
> जघान । तथार्णवाभमौर्णवाभनामकमहीशुवमहीशुवनामकं च शत्रुमहन् ।
> तथा हिमेन तुषारेणोदकेन वार्बुदं मेघमविध्यत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Indra, equal in brilliance, killed the enemy named Vṛtra; likewise he killed the enemies named Aurṇavābha and Ahīśuva. And with frost, that is, with snow-water, he pierced the cloud called Arbuda.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- ऋचीषमः (*ṛcīṣamaḥ*)—the shining Indra
- वृत्रं (*vṛtraṃ*)—the asura called Vṛtra
- अहन् (*ahan*)—killed (and)
- और्णवाभं (*aurṇavābhaṃ*)—the asura called Aurṇavābha
- अहीशुवं (*ahīśuvaṃ*)—and the asura called Ahīśuva he killed
- अर्बुदं (*arbudaṃ*)—the cloud
- हिमेन (*himena*)—with cold water or with frost
- अविध्यत् (*avidhyat*)—he pierced

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

Indra, whose body shines exceedingly, killed the asura called Vṛtra and the asuras Aurṇavābha and Ahīśuva. And with frost he split the cloud so that it was torn open.

##### English translation
The brilliant Indra slew Vritra, Aurnavabha, Ahishuva; he smote Arbuda with frost.

#### Ṛk 27

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> प्र व॑ उ॒ग्राय॑ नि॒ष्टुरेऽषा॑ळ्हाय प्रस॒क्षिणे॑ ।  
> दे॒वत्तं॒ ब्रह्म॑ गायत ॥ २७ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> प्र । वः । उग्राय । निःतुरे । अषाळ्याय । प्रसक्षिणे ।  
> देवत्तं । ब्रह्म । गायत ॥ २७ ॥

### Printed p. 253

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे उद्गातारः वो ययमुग्रायोद्दूर्णाय निष्टुरे शत्रून्निस्तरतेऽषाळ्दाय शत्रूणामभिभवित्रे प्रसक्षिणे प्रसहनशीलायेंद्राय देवत्तं देवप्रसादलब्धं ब्रह्म स्तोत्रं प्र गायत ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Udgātṛs, to Indra, fierce, that is, exceedingly strong; the अषाळ्हाय (*aṣāḷhāya*), the overcomer of enemies; the प्रसक्षिणे (*prasakṣiṇe*), of an overpowering nature; sing forth the hymn that is gift of the gods, that is, obtained through the favour of the gods.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वः (*vaḥ*)—(O Udgātṛs who sing the Sāman) you
- उग्राय (*ugrāya*)—to the fierce
- निस्तुरे (*nisture*)—who disregards enemies
- अषाळ्हाय (*aṣāḷhāya*)—who defeats enemies
- प्रसक्षिणे (*prasakṣiṇe*)—who is able to bear the violence of enemies in battle, to Indra
- देवत्तं (*devattaṃ*)—obtained through the favour of the gods
- ब्रह्म (*brahma*)—the hymn
- प्र गायत (*pra gāyata*)—recite with song

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O priests called Udgātṛs, who sing the Sāman, to Indra, who is terrible to enemies, who in battles and the like disregards the enemies and defeats them, and who is able to bear their violence, sing and recite the Sāman mantras (hymns) obtained through the favour of the gods.

##### English translation
Sing aloud (priests) to the fierce, Victorious (Indra) the overpowerer, the subduer (of foes), the praise inspired by the gods.

#### Ṛk 28

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> यो विश्वा॑न्य॒भि व्र॒ता सोम॑स्य॒ मदे॒ अंध॑सः ।  
> इंद्रो॑ दे॒वेषु॒ चेत॑ति ॥ २८ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> यः । विश्वानि । अभि । व्रता । सोमस्य । मदे । अंधसः ।  
> इंद्रः । देवेषु । चेतति ॥ २८ ॥

### Printed p. 254

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> अंधसोऽद्यमानस्य सोमस्य मदे संजाते विश्वानि सर्वाणि व्रता व्रतानि
> कर्माणि य इंद्रो देवेष्वभि चेतति ज्ञापयति तस्मा इंद्राय देवत्तं ब्रह्म
> गायतेत्यर्थः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

In the exhilaration that arises from the Soma-food being consumed, Indra makes known among the gods all pious acts (व्रता (*vratā*) means rites). The sense is: to that Indra sing the hymn obtained through the favour of the gods.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- अंधसः (*aṃdhasaḥ*)—of the Soma fit to be eaten (fit to be drunk)
- सोमस्य (*somasya*)—of the Soma juice
- मदे (*made*)—when exhilaration has arisen
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Indra
- विश्वानि (*viśvāni*)—all
- व्रता (*vratā*)—deeds of valour
- देवेषु (*deveṣu*)—among the gods
- अभि चेतति (*abhi cetati*)—makes known; sing the Sāman to such an Indra

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Udgātṛs, when Indra is exhilarated by the Soma juice fit to be drunk, that Indra makes many deeds of valour known to the gods. Praise such an Indra by singing Sāman mantras in the form of hymns addressed to him.

##### English translation
Indra who, in the exhilaration of the quaffed Soma, makes known among the gods all pious rites.

#### Ṛk 29

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> इ॒ह त्या स॑ध॒माद्या॒ हरी॒ हिर॑ण्यकेश्या ।  
> वो॒ळ्ळाम॒भि प्रयो॑ हि॒तं ॥ २९ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> इह । त्या । सधमाद्या । हरी इति । हिरण्यकेश्या ।  
> वोळ्ळां । अभि । प्रयः । हितं ॥ २९ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> इह । त्या । सध्वमाद्या । हरी इति । हिरण्यकेश्या ।
> वोळ्हां । अभि । प्रयः । हितं ॥ २९ ॥
> इह यज्ञे त्या तौ प्रसिद्धौ सधमाद्या सह माद्यंतौ हिरण्यकेश्या हिरण्यकेश्यौ
> हरी अश्वा हितं हितकरं प्रयः सोमरूपमन्नमभ्यभिलक्ष्य वोळ्हां । इंद्रं
> वहतां । प्रापयतामिति ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

Here, in this sacrifice, those two well-known horses of Indra, who rejoice together (सधमाद्या (*sadhamādyā*)) and are golden-maned (हिरण्यकेश्या (*hiraṇyakeśyā*)), are to bring Indra here, conveying him towards the beneficial food, that is, the food in the form of Soma; "let them carry" means let them bring Indra.

### Printed p. 255

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- इह (*iha*)—in this sacrifice
- त्या (*tyā*)—the well-known (two)
- सधमाद्या (*sadhamādyā*)—who are always together and rejoicing
- हिरण्यकेश्या (*hiraṇyakeśyā*)—shining with manes that glitter like gold
- हरी (*harī*)—the two horses of Indra
- हितं (*hitaṃ*)—beneficial
- प्रयः अभि (*prayaḥ abhi*)—towards the food in the form of Soma, with the desire for it (in order to eat it)
- वॊळ्तां (*vòḷtāṃ*)—let them bring Indra **NOTE**

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

May those well-known horses of Indra, who are always together and happy, adorned with a row of hair on the neck that glitters like gold, bring Indra to this sacrificial ground, towards the food in the form of Soma.

##### English translation
May those two golden-maned steeds, together exulting, bring him hither to the salutaey (sacrificial) food.

#### Ṛk 30

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> अ॒र्वांचं॑ त्वा पुरुष्टुत प्रि॒यमे॑धस्तुता॒ हरी॑ ।  
> सो॒म॒पेया॑य वक्षतः ॥ ३० ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> अर्वाचं । त्वा । पुरुस्तुत । प्रियमेधस्तुता । हरी इति ।  
> सोमपेयाय । वक्षतः ॥ ३० ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे पुरष्टुतेंद्र त्वा त्वां प्रियमेधस्तुता हरी अश्वा सोमपेयाय
> सोमपानायार्वांचमस्मदभिमुखं वक्षतः । वहतः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, praised by many, your two horses, praised by Priyamedha, are to carry you towards us for the drinking of Soma.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- पुरुषत (*puruṣata*)—O Indra, praised by many
- त्वा (*tvā*)—you
- प्रियमेधस्तुता (*priyamedhastutā*)—praised by me, who am named Priyamedha
- हरी (*harī*)—the horses
- सोमपेयाय (*somapeyāya*)—for the drinking of Soma
- अर्वांचं (*arvāṃcaṃ*)—facing us
- वक्षतः (*vakṣataḥ*)—let them bring

### Printed p. 256

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, praised by many people, may your horses, praised by me, who am famed by the name Priyamedha, bring you to us, facing us, for the drinking of Soma.

##### English translation
Indra, the glorified of many, let your horses, praised by Priyamedha, bring you down to drink the Soma.

## Sūkta 33 (Maṇḍala 8)
**NOTE**

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> वयं घ त्वेत्येकोनविंशत्यृचं तृतीयं सूक्तं काण्वस्य मेध्यातिथेरार्षं
> बृहतीछंदस्कं। षोडश्याद्यास्ति स्रो गायत्र्य एकोनविंश्यनुष्टुप् । इंद्रो देवता।
> तथा चानुक्रांतं । वयं फैकोना मेध्यातिथिर्भार्हतं त्रिगायत्र्यनुष्टुबंतमिति॥
> महाव्रते निष्केवल्ये बार्हततृचाशीतावादितः पंचदशर्चः । तथैव पंचमारण्यके
> शौनकेन सूत्रते । वयं घ त्वा सुतावंत इति पंचदश नो షు त्वा नाघतश्चनेत्येतस्य
> द्विपदां चोद्धरति । ऐ. आ. ५-२-४ । इति ॥ चातुर्विंशिकेऽहनि माध्यंदिनसवने
> ब्राह्मणाच्छंसिशस्त्रे वयं घ त्वेति तृचो वैकल्पिकः स्तोत्रियः । तथा च सूत्रं ।
> वयं घ त्वा सुतावंतः क ईं वेद सुते सचा । आ. ७-४ । इति ॥ स्वरसाम्ययमेव
> तृचोऽनुरूपः । सूत्रितं च । वयं घ त्वा सुतावंत इति तिस्रो बृहत्यः । आ. ८-५ ।
> इति ॥ तस्मिन्नेव शस्त्रे क ईं वेदेति वैकल्पिकोऽनुरूपः । सूत्रमुक्तमेव ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

The hymn beginning वयं घ त्वा (*vayaṃ gha tvā*) is the third hymn, of nineteen Ṛks, with Medhyātithi Kāṇva as seer, in the Bṛhatī metre; the three Ṛks beginning with the sixteenth are Gāyatrī, and the nineteenth is an Anuṣṭubh. Indra is the deity. The Anukramaṇī says the same: Medhyātithi, Bārhata, three Gāyatrīs, ending with an Anuṣṭubh. Use: in the Mahāvrata, in the Niṣkevalya, the first fifteen Ṛks are used as Bārhata tṛcas **NOTE**. Śaunaka states this in the fifth Āraṇyaka (Ai. Ā. 5-2-4) **NOTE**. On the Cāturviṃśika day, at the midday pressing, in the Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin's recitation, the three Ṛks beginning वयं घ त्वा (*vayaṃ gha tvā*) are an optional Stotriya tṛca; the sūtra says वयं घ त्वा सुतावंतः क ईं वेद सुते सचा (*vayaṃ gha tvā sutāvaṃtaḥ ka īṃ veda sute sacā*) (Ā. 7-4). In the same way this tṛca is the Anurūpa (the bhāṣya reads स्वरसाम्ययमेव (*svarasāmyayameva*) **NOTE**); the sūtra says वयं घ त्वा सुतावंत इति तिस्रो बृहत्यः (*vayaṃ gha tvā sutāvaṃta iti tisro bṛhatyaḥ*) (Ā. 8-5). In that same recitation the tṛca क ईं वेद (*ka īṃ veda*) is the optional Anurūpa; the sūtra has been quoted. **NOTE**

##### Anuvāda

This hymn, beginning वयं घ त्वा (*vayaṃ gha tvā*), is the third hymn in the fifth Anuvāka. It has nineteen Ṛks. Its seer is Medhyātithi Kāṇva; the deity is Indra; the metre is Bṛhatī, but the three Ṛks from the sixteenth onwards are in the Gāyatrī metre. The Anukramaṇī says: वयं फैकोना मेध्यातिथिर्बार्हतं त्रिगायत्र्यनुष्टुबंतं (*vayaṃ phaikonā medhyātithirbārhataṃ trigāyatryanuṣṭubaṃtaṃ*). In the Mahāvrata rite, at the time of reciting the Niṣkevalya śastra mantras, the first fifteen Ṛks of this hymn are applied to the Ṛks of Bṛhatī metre, as is expounded by the sage Śaunaka in the fifth Āraṇyaka of the Aitareya Āraṇyaka by the sūtra वयं घ त्वा सुतावंत इति पंचदश मो षु त्वा वाघतश्च नेत्येतस्य द्विपदां चोद्धरति (*vayaṃ gha tvā sutāvaṃta iti paṃcadaśa mo ṣu tvā vāghataśca netyetasya dvipadāṃ coddharati*) (Ai. Ā. 5-2-4). In the day called Cāturviṃśika of the sacrifice, at the time of the midday pressing, among the śastra mantras that the priest called Brāhmaṇācchaṃsin has to recite, the three Ṛks beginning with वयं घ त्वा (*vayaṃ gha tvā*) are to be recited as the optional Stotriya tṛca
### Printed p. 257
as is expounded by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, वयं घ त्वा सुतावंतः क ईं वेद सुते सजा (*vayaṃ gha tvā sutāvaṃtaḥ ka īṃ veda sute sajā*) (Ā. 2-4). In the rite called स्वरसाम्मियॆंब (*svarasāmmiyèṃba*) too, these three Ṛks are to be recited as the Anurūpa tṛca, as expounded by the Āśvalāyana Śrauta Sūtra, वयं घ त्वा सुतावंतॆ इति तिस्रो बृहत्यः (*vayaṃ gha tvā sutāvaṃtè iti tisro bṛhatyaḥ*) (Ā. 2-5). In the śastra mantras of the same rite, the tṛca beginning क ईं वेद (*ka īṃ veda*) (the Ṛks 7 to 9 of this hymn) is to be recited as the Anurūpa tṛca; the sūtra that lays this down (Ā. 2-4) is thus indicated.

##### Particulars

- Maṇḍala: 8
- Anuvāka: 5
- Sūkta: 33
- Aṣṭaka: 6
- Adhyāya: 3
- Varga: 7, 8, 9, 10
- Number of Ṛks in the hymn: 19
- Ṛṣi: Medhātithi Kāṇva
- Devatā: Indra
- Chandas: Ṛks 1 to 15 Bṛhatī; Ṛks 16 to 18 Gāyatrī; Ṛk 19 Anuṣṭubh

#### Ṛk 1

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> व॒यं घ॑ त्वा सु॒तावं॑त॒ आपो॒ न वृ॒क्तब॑र्हिषः ।  
> प॒वित्र॑स्य प्र॒स्रव॑णेषु वृत्रह॒न्परि॑ स्तो॒तार॑ आसते ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> वयं । घ । त्वा । सुतवंतः । आपः । न । वृक्तबर्हिषः ।  
> पवित्रस्य । प्रस्रवणेषु । वृत्तहन् । परि । स्तोतारः । आसते ॥ १ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वृत्रहन्निंद्र त्वा त्वां वयं घ वयं खलु सुतवंतः सोममभिषुतवंत
> आपो नाप इव प्रवणमभिगच्छामः । पवित्रस्य सोमानां प्रस्रवणेषु व्यक्तबर्हिषः
> स्तीर्णबर्हिषः स्तोतारश्च त्वां पर्युपासते ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Indra, slayer of Vṛtra, we, who have pressed the Soma, come to you as waters go to a slope. And at the flowing of the pure Soma, the praisers who have strewn the grass (व्यक्तबर्हिषः (*vyaktabarhiṣaḥ*), glossed स्तीर्णबर्हिषः (*stīrṇabarhiṣaḥ*), "with grass spread") sit around and attend you.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- वृत्रहन् (*vṛtrahan*)—O Indra, slayer of Vṛtra
- त्वा (*tvā*)—with reference to you
- वयं (*vayaṃ*)—we
- सुतवंतः (*sutavaṃtaḥ*)—pressing the Soma juice
- आपोन (*āpona*)—like water that flows down to a low-lying entrance
### Printed p. 258
- पवित्रस्य (*pavitrasya*)—of the pure Soma
- प्रस्रवणेषु (*prasravaṇeṣu*)—when it has been pressed and prepared; we are performing the pressing of Soma
- व्यक्तबर्हिषः (*vyaktabarhiṣaḥ*)—who have spread, as a seat for you to come and sit in the sacrifice, the cut darbha grass and prepared it
- स्तोत्रारः (*stotrāraḥ*)—the priests who are reciting hymns, attend you

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, slayer of Vṛtra, just as water flows to low ground and comes to rest, the Soma juice that we press for you flows in the sacrifice that we perform, gathers together and comes to rest in one place. When such Soma has been prepared for you, the priests, having made ready a seat of darbha grass for your coming and sitting, serve you with hymns and the like.

##### English translation
We are pouring forth to you the Soma juice like water: the praisers strewing the clipped sacred grass, worship you, slayer of Vritra, in the, streams (falling) from the filter.

#### Ṛk 2

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> स्वरं॑ति त्वा सु॒ते नरो॒ वसो॑ निरे॒क उ॒क्किनः॑ ।  
> क॒दा सु॒तं तृ॑षा॒ण ओक॒ आ ग॑म॒ इंद्र॑ स्व॒ब्दीव॒ वंस॑गः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> स्वरंति । त्वा । सुते । नरः । वसो इति । निरेके । उक्किनः ।  
> कदा । सुतं । तृषाणः । ओकः । आ । गमः । इंद्र । स्वब्दीऽइव । वंसगः ॥ २ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे वसो वासयितरिंद्र त्वा त्वां सुतेऽभिषुते सोमे निरेके निर्गमन
> उक्तिनो नरो नेतारः स्वरंति । शब्दायंते । अपि चेंद्रः सुतं सोमं प्रति
> तृषाणस्तृष्यन् स्वद्बीव स्वभूतशब्द इव वंसगो वननीयगमनो वृषभः शब्दं
> कुर्वन् तदौकः स्थानमागमत् ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Vasu, bestower of dwelling, Indra, when the Soma has been pressed, at its outflow (निर्गमन (*nirgamana*), "coming forth"), the leaders, the priests who speak praises, sound your praise. And Indra, thirsty for the pressed Soma, like a bull of lovely gait that utters a roar, came to that abode (स्थानमागमत् (*sthānamāgamat*), "to the place").

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे वसो (*he vaso*)—O Indra, giver of dwelling
- सुते (*sute*)—the Soma juice, the creepers having been crushed and pressed
- निरेके (*nireke*)—as it is flowing fresh
- उक्तिनः (*uktinaḥ*)—reciting the hymns that have been spoken
- वरः (*varaḥ*)—the priests and others who take the chief parts in the sacrifice **NOTE**
- त्वां (*tvāṃ*)—you
- स्वरंति (*svaraṃti*)—praise with a loud voice (such an Indra)
- सुतं (*sutaṃ*)—the pressed and prepared Soma juice, desiring it
- तृषाणः (*tṛṣāṇaḥ*)—being thirsty
- स्वद्व वंसगः (*svadva vaṃsagaḥ*)—bellowing like a bull
- कदा (*kadā*)—when
- ओकः (*okaḥ*)—to this place where the sacrifice is performed
- आ गमः (*ā gamaḥ*)—will he come?

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, giver of dwelling, when the Soma creepers are crushed with a stone, the Soma juice that comes out of them flows. At that time the priests and sacrificers who lead the sacrifice praise you with a firm, loud voice. When will such an Indra, thirsty like a bellowing bull, come to this sacrificial place for the drinking of Soma?

##### English translation
The leaders (of rites), repeating praises, shout to you, Vasu, when the libation issues forth; when, Indra, bellowing like a bull, do you come to the dwelling thirsting for the Soma?

#### Ṛk 3

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> कण्व॑भिर्धृष्ण॒वा धृ॒षद्वाजं॑ दर्षि सह॒स्रिणं॑ ।  
> पि॒शंग॑रूपं मघवन्वि चर्षणे म॒क्षु गोमं॑तमीमहे ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> कण्वभिः । धृष्णो इति । आ । धृषत् । वाजं । दर्षि । सहस्रिणं ।  
> पिशंगरूपं । मघवन् । विचर्षणे । मक्षु । गोमंतं । ईमहे ॥ ३ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे धृष्णो धर्षकेंद्र कण्वेभिः कण्णानुद्दिश्य ॥ विभक्तिव्यत्ययः ॥ सहस्रिणं
> सहस्रसंख्याकं वाजमा दर्षि । देहि । हे मघवन्थनवन् विचर्षणे विद्रष्टरिंद्र
> धृषद्धृष्टं पिशंगरूपं गोमंतं च वाजं मक्षु शीघ्रमीमहे । याचामहे ।
> श्वामिति शेषः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O bold Indra, assailant of foes, give to the Kaṇvas (the Sanskrit has कण्वेभिः (*kaṇvebhiḥ*), glossed as "with reference to the Kaṇvas", an interchange of case) food numbering a thousand. O wealthy Indra, far-seeing, we quickly ask for food that is bold, tawny in form and rich in cattle; the word "you" is to be supplied. **NOTE**

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- हे धृष्णो (*he dhṛṣṇo*)—O Indra, assailant of foes
- कण्णेभिः (*kaṇṇebhiḥ*)—with reference to the sons of the sage Kaṇva
- सहस्रिणं (*sahasriṇaṃ*)—numbering a thousand (much)
- वाजं (*vājaṃ*)—food
- आ दर्षि (*ā darṣi*)—give
- मघवन् (*maghavan*)—wealthy
- विचर्षणे (*vicarṣaṇe*)—O Indra, who sees and knows everything
- धृषक् (*dhṛṣak*)—that which gives strength
- पिशंगरूपं (*piśaṃgarūpaṃ*)—that which has ripened (matured) and turned yellow in colour
- गोमंतं (*gomaṃtaṃ*)—barley
- मक्षु (*makṣu*)—quickly
- ईमहे (*īmahe*)—we pray to obtain from you

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)

O Indra, assailant of foes, give to us, the descendants of the sage Kaṇva, food and eatables numbering a thousand, that is, of many kinds or in abundance. O Indra, who sees all and are wealthy, we pray to you to give us quickly barley (rice), which is nourishing, well ripened and yellow in colour.

##### English translation
Resolute (Indra) bestow abundantly upon the descendants of Kauwa thousands of viands; wise Maghavan, we earnestly solicit you for (wealth) of gold and cattle.

#### Ṛk 4

##### ॥ संहितापाठः ॥ (Saṃhitā-pāṭha)

> पा॒हि गायांध॑सो॒ मद॒ इंद्रा॑य मेध्यातिथे ।  
> यः संमि॑श्लो॒ हर्यो॒र्यः सु॒ते सचा॑ व॒ज्री रथो॑ हिर॒  ॑
> ण्ययः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ पदपाठः ॥ (Pada-pāṭha)

> पाहि । गाय । अंधसः । मदे । इंद्राय । मेध्यआतिथे !  
> यः । संमिश्लः । हर्योः । यः । सुते । सचा । वज्री । रथः । हिरण्ययः ॥ ४ ॥

##### ॥ सायणभाष्यम् ॥ (Sāyaṇa-bhāṣya)

> हे मेध्यातिथे पाहि । सोमं पिब । अंधसः पीतस्य सोमस्य मदे तस्मा
> इंद्राय गाय । स्तोत्रं पठ च । य इंद्रो हर्योरश्वयोः संमिश्लः स्वरथे
> संमिश्लयिता । यश्च सुते सोमे सचा सहायः । य इंद्रो वज्रे । यस्य रथो
> हिरण्ययो हिरण्मयः ॥

##### Gist of the bhāṣya

O Medhyātithi, drink the Soma. In the exhilaration of the drunk Soma, sing, that is, recite a hymn, to that Indra who joins his two horses to his own chariot; who is a companion at the pressed Soma; who bears the thunderbolt **NOTE**; and whose chariot is golden, that is, made of gold.

##### प्रतिपदार्थ (Pratipadārtha)

- मेध्यातिथे (*medhyātithe*)—O Medhyātithi
- पाहि (*pāhi*)—drink the Soma
- अंथगः (*aṃthagaḥ*)—from the drinking of Soma
- मदे (*made*)—when satisfaction has arisen
- यः (*yaḥ*)—which Indra
- हर्योः (*haryoḥ*)—his two horses

### Printed p. 261
- संमिश्लः (*saṃmiślaḥ*)—yokes to his chariot
- यः (*yaḥ*)—who
- सुते (*sute*)—when the Soma juice has been pressed
- सचा (*sacā*)—comes and joins with me
- वज्री (*vajrī*)—who holds the thunderbolt
- रथः (*rathaḥ*)—whose chariot
- हिरण्ययः (*hiraṇyayaḥ*)—is made of gold; to that Indra
- इंद्राय (*iṃdrāya*)—to that Indra
- गाय (*gāya*)—recite a hymn

##### भावार्थ (Bhāvārtha)
